Surface CoHAs and ADE Yangians
Let 𝑆Γ →ℂ2/Γ be the minimal resolution for a finite subgroup Γ ⊂SL2(ℂ). The exceptional curves
have ADE intersection matrix. Moduli of sheaves supported on these curves carry convolution corre-
spondences.
Theorem 94.1 (Curve-supported surface CoHA). The completed positive CoHA of one-dimensional
sheaves supported on the exceptional curves is identiﬁed with a nonstandard positive half of the aﬃne
Yangian of the corresponding ADE Lie algebra.
Proof. The theorem is the curve-supported surface construction of Diaconescu–Porta–Sala–Schiffmann–
Vasserot [25]. The simple curve sheaves give the simple generators, Hecke correspondences give the current
operators, and the completed PBW comparison gives the affine Yangian presentation.
This theorem is a local K3 model whenever an ADE configuration occurs on a K3 surface. The global
K3 surface adds compact curve classes and monodromy beyond the local root lattice.
257




CHAPTER
95
Coulomb branches and shifted Yangians
For a three-dimensional N = 4 gauge theory, the BFN Coulomb branch is defined by equivariant
Borel–Moore homology of an affine-Grassmannian correspondence. Its convolution algebra quantizes
the Coulomb branch [11]. In quiver gauge theories, shifted Yangians and their truncations provide
presentations of these quantizations. Boundary conditions select the shift and truncation data.
The BFN construction supplies a second geometric source of Yangians. Its grading and coproduct
arise from loop rotation and convolution, while the surface CoHA source arises from sheaf extensions.
A comparison between the two is a model-specific equivalence.

Local ADE Yangians
An ADE configuration of (−2)-curves on 𝑆gives a root sublattice of 𝐻2(𝑆, ℤ). Sheaves supported
on the configuration produce the surface CoHA of the preceding part and hence a completed positive
affine ADE Yangian. Nakajima quiver varieties supply compatible Yangian actions on their equivariant
cohomology.
Thus K3 geometry contains local nonabelian Yangian sectors indexed by ADE enhancements of the
Picard lattice. Picard monodromy transports the ADE root sublattices over the corresponding Noether–
Lefschetz loci. A family of sheaf moduli and Hecke correspondences compatible with that monodromy
transports the Yangian actions themselves.

CHAPTER

Physical meaning
The protected avatar is the complete universal algebraic model selected by the Igusa root datum. The
Ginzburg category supplies its noncommutative three-Calabi–Yau sector. The quiver Hall category
supplies its ordinary extension geometry. The Borcherds root superspace supplies its protected one-
particle states. The colored Ran object supplies its local and wrapped current algebra on the elliptic
curve. Hong’s quantum Borcherds algebra supplies its line-operator symmetry. The root Dirac family
supplies its first-order determinant, and the Fock construction supplies the scalar square.
A compact string construction with specified simple-root states and extension amplitudes maps to
the protected avatar through the presentation universal property. The comparison is determined on
primitives; PBW propagates it to multiparticle states, the Hall product, the current algebra, the quantum
double, and the determinant.

CHAPTER

Universal property of the protected avatar
A presentation-preserving protected theory consists of a three-Calabi–Yau category, an oriented Hall
boundary, a colored elliptic factorization algebra, a quasitriangular quantum symmetry, an oriented
determinant, and comparison functors from the Borcherds generators-and-relations construction to
these five objects. The chosen simple-root states satisfy the Gritsenko–Nikulin Cartan, parity, and Serre
relations.
Theorem 109.1 (Presentation universal property). Every presentation-preserving protected theory carries
a unique primitive Lie-superalgebra morphism
𝔫Δ5,+ −→Prim(𝔗)
extending the chosen simple-root states. The Hall, Ginzburg, current, quantum-group, and determinant
functors extend this primitive morphism to the ﬁve selected comparison maps. A primitive isomorphism
together with equivalences of the selected functorial realizations gives an equivalence of protected data.
Proof. The Borcherds generators and relations give the unique primitive morphism. Each remaining construction
is functorial in a presentation map: quiver Hall convolution in the quiver map, Calabi–Yau completion in the
dg-algebra map, chiral Chevalley theory in the Lie map, Hong quantization in the Cartan superdatum, and the
diagonal Fredholm operator in the root-graded map. PBW promotes the primitive isomorphism to the completed
enveloping and Fock objects.

From positive half to full quantum group
Suppose the compact or local positive algebra 𝐻+ is paired continuously with a negative half 𝐻−, carries
a completed coproduct, and admits Cartan data and a separated triangular completion. The completed
double
U = b𝐷(𝐻+)
has triangular decomposition 𝐻−b⊗𝐻0 b⊗𝐻+. Its universal 𝑅-matrix acts on continuous tensor prod-
ucts of modules. The classical limit is a Lie bialgebra whose cobracket is the first-order term of the
coproduct asymmetry.
For ℂ3, 𝐻+ is the positive affine Yangian. For local ADE surfaces, it is the completed positive affine

exists and that the canonical comparison functor to finite-dimensional 𝐻-comodules is comonadic. Ten-
sor product makes 𝐻a bialgebra, and rigidity supplies its antipode. A braiding gives a coquasitriangular
form; a meromorphic braiding gives its spectral version.
Theorem 112.1 (Rigid Tannaka reconstruction). Under the preceding ﬁnite and comonadic hypotheses,
the comparison functor is a monoidal equivalence
L ≃Comodfd(𝐻).
Braiding transports to the universal coquasitriangular form on 𝐻.
Proof. Matrix coefficients define the universal 𝐻-coaction on every 𝜔(𝑀). Tensor product and duality give
the bialgebra and antipode structures. The Barr–Beck comonadic theorem identifies L with coalgebras for the
induced comonad, and the coend identifies that comonad with 𝐻⊗−. The hexagon identities transport to the
coquasitriangular identities.
This reconstruction explains why a line-operator category, a fiber functor, and exchange together
produce a quantum group. The ordered boundary algebra alone provides fusion; the plane of exchange
supplies braiding.

The centre and the double
The Hochschild centre 𝑍ord(𝐴) is the universal E2 algebra acting centrally on an ordered algebra. The
Drinfeld centre 𝑍(Mod𝐴) is the braided category of modules equipped with half-braidings. Under

endomorphisms of the identity. Derived loop-space descriptions of centres provide the geometric form
of this relation [6].
The centre andthe double answercomplementaryquestions. The centre classifies centralbulkactions.
The double reconstructs a full quantum group from paired positive and negative halves. A field theory
can supply a comparison by realizing both as line and bulk operator constructions.

Tree exactness and the quantum master
equation
Every interaction vertex in (115) has one 𝐵-leg and two 𝐴-legs. The propagator pairs an 𝐴-leg with a
𝐵-leg.
Lemma 116.1 (Loop bound). A connected Feynman graph of the cotangent theory has loop number at most
one.
Proof. A graph with𝑉interaction vertices has exactly𝑉internal or external 𝐵-half-edges. Every internal edge
consumes one of them, hence 𝐸≤𝑉. The loop number is 𝐸−𝑉+ 1 ≤1.
The positive algebra is graded by strictly positive root height. For homogeneous 𝑥∈𝔫Δ5,+(𝐻), the
operator ad𝑥raises height. Its coadjoint operator lowers dual height. A product of adjoint operators
labelled by positive roots has total nonzero height shift and therefore has zero trace.
Theorem 116.2 (Perturbative exactness). Every one-loop Lie weight of the ﬁnite-height theory vanishes.
The renormalized quantum master equation holds with zero counterterm, and the perturbative effective
action is represented by tree graphs. The statement is compatible with the height maps 𝐻→𝐻+ 1.
Proof. The loop bound leaves tree graphs and one-loop graphs. Their Lie factor is a cyclic trace of a product of
adjoint or coadjoint operators. Positive height makes each such product strictly triangular, so its trace is zero. The
anomaly and the one-loop effective vertex therefore vanish. Tree graphs satisfy the classical master equation by the
usual boundary-stratum argument. Every map preserves root height.
Corollary 116.3 (Height-complete quantum source). The coeﬃcientwise inverse limit
ObsΔ

Scalarization hierarchy
The constructions descend through four levels:
factorization or Hall object −→primitive Lie algebra
−→PBW character −→scalar partition function.
Each arrow retains the quotient of structure displayed at its target. The first object contains supports,
extension maps, and brackets. The second retains the infinitesimal algebra. The third retains signed
graded dimensions. The fourth adds a trace and normalization. Upward reconstruction is supplied by
the corresponding geometric operations and comparison morphisms.

The foundational theorem
Definition 128.1 (Complete Calabi–Yau quantum-group datum). A complete datum is a governing
tuple 𝔇together with:
(i) PTVV-representable moduli for the smooth proper Calabi–Yau category;
(ii) a renormalized BV quantization satisfying the quantum master equation and a local compact
factorization generator 𝑏;
(iii) a boundary reduction or proper locally trivial map to a curve satisfying factorization pushforward,
together with the supplied curve-level faces and aligned distributive law;
(iv) a strong-oriented Hall coefficient theory whose pull–push maps satisfy base change and whose
positive algebra is separated and complete;
(v) paired negative and Cartan halves, a completed coproduct, a continuous nondegenerate Hopf
pairing, and a separated triangular completion on which the canonical tensors converge;
(vi) compatible open–closed and Hall–boundary morphisms;
(vii) a parity-refined primitive comparison for every automorphic identification.
Theorem 128.2 (Calabi–Yau quantum-group assembly). Let a complete datum consist of:
(i) a Calabi–Yau category and a representable moduli-of-objects problem;
(ii) a quantized cyclic elliptic BV theory and a boundary or defect generator;
(iii) a Hall extension correspondence with coeﬃcient functor, base-change theorem, strong orientation
data, and separated charge completion;
(iv) paired positive and negative bialgebras, Cartan data, a nondegenerate continuous Hopf pairing,
and a completed double;
(v) every curve-level distributive, PBW, and analytic comparison used below.
Then the datum determines functorially:
(i) framed E2 Hochschild cochains and the shifted-symplectic moduli structure;
(ii) the three curve-level algebraic faces and their three bars;
(iii) the positive Hall/BPS algebra and its primitive Lie algebra;
(iv) the completed quantum double and braided representation category;
339


128. The foundational theorem
340
(v) every supplied comparison among categorical, ﬁeld-theoretic, Hall, current, quantum, and automor-
phic realizations;
(vi) the Weyl-shifted PBW/Chevalley character of any parity-reﬁned primitive Lie-superalgebra identi-
ﬁcation.
Proof. Cyclic Deligne andshiftedsymplectic geometry give the firstoutput[9,10,63]. Boundary factorization gives
the curve faces, and Volume I gives their bars and centres. The Hall correspondence and its base-change theorem
give the associative product; strong orientation supplies the coefficient coherence required in the 3-Calabi–Yau
setting [49]. The paired bialgebra data give the double and universal 𝑅-matrix. The comparison maps commute
by hypothesis. PBW and Chevalley antisymmetrization give the final character. Each construction is natural under
a morphism preserving the displayed data.
The local quiver, equivariant surface, K3 correspondence, and Igusa Borcherds chapters construct
objects in their stated categories. A geometric 𝐾3 × 𝐸Hall theory determines a morphism from its
primitive and determinant-line data to the universal Igusa objects.

Closure theorem for the established and
universal layers
Theorem 130.1 (Foundational closure). The following layers are complete mathematical constructions:
(i) framed Hochschild and shifted-symplectic categorical data;
(ii) perturbative factorization observables from a quantized cyclic elliptic BV theory;
(iii) the three curve-level algebraic faces and their bars;
(iv) Hall products in the established quiver, surface, 2-Calabi–Yau, and strong-oriented 3-Calabi–Yau
settings;
(v) the K3 Heisenberg, LLV, and local ADE Yangian symmetry atlas;
(vi) the Igusa Borcherds root algebra, its quantized Borcherds superalgebra, proper-height bar/current
system, classical local BF theory, exact ﬁnite zero-mode BV theory, and analytic determinant;
(vii) the primitive reduced 𝐾3 × 𝐸scalar character.
A compact 𝐾3 × 𝐸quantum-group realization is exactly an instance of the compact comparison theorem
with a constructed reduced Hall datum.
Proof. Items (1)–(7) are the theorems cited and proved in the corresponding chapters. The last sentence is the
definition of a compact realization together with the preceding recognition theorem.

CHAPTER
132
Concordance with the original
Calabi–Yau volume
The reconstitution preserves the original vision of a bridge among Calabi–Yau categories, holomorphic
field theory, chiral algebras, Hall algebras, quantum groups, and automorphic products. It realizes that
vision through the following replacements:
(i) framed E2 Hochschild cochains and (2 −𝑑)-shifted symplectic moduli replace a dimension-
dependent Hochschild E𝑛assertion;
(ii) a geometric BV theory and boundary category produce factorization observables and transverse
ordered chiral algebras; associative chiral convolution is recorded as a separate supplied structure;
(iii) pushforward along an actual map 𝑝: 𝑋→𝐶replaces formal cycle-specialization notation;
(iv) extension stacks, coefficient sheaves, orientations, and completions define the Hall algebra;
(v) coproduct, Cartan, Hopf pairing, and topology define the double;
(vi) K3 Heisenberg, LLV orthogonal symmetry, local ADE Yangians, and global BPS generalized Kac–
Moody algebras occupy distinct strata joined by explicit comparison maps where constructed;
(vii) the graph of the 𝐾3 × 𝐸Gram map becomes multiplicative in the polarized central extension;
(viii) point-local and wrapped sectors form a hybrid factorization problem;
(ix) the Igusa form is the Weyl-shifted PBW/Chevalley character of 𝔤Δ5, and compact Hall geometry
maps to it through the primitive comparison;
(x) anomaly identities are transported through explicit maps between deformation complexes.
These constructions retain the scale of the original program while placing every operation in its natural
category.

CHAPTER

The three K3 brackets
The Heisenberg bracket is the excess intersection of two Nakajima incidence kernels. The LLV bracket
is the commutator of diagonal Lefschetz kernels and their adjoints. The local Yangian bracket is the
convolution commutator of ADE Hecke kernels with spectral equivariance. In Corr𝐾3(𝑆) all three are
compositions of geometric kernels. Their mixed commutators are the corresponding triple-intersection
classes. The action algebra and Tannaka coend retain these classes while preserving the three geometric
constructions.

Exact arithmetic checks
The companion verification calculation establishes:
(i) the low Fourier rows of 𝜙0,1 through 𝑞3 by exact theta-series division;
(ii) the vector-valued cocycle identity for the Gram polarization;
(iii) the rational Yang–Baxter equation on (ℂ2)⊗3;
(iv) the 𝐴2 Hall flag counts at ranks zero and one;
(v) the type-II Gram matrix and reflection identities.
Each calculation uses integer or rational arithmetic and appears as a worked formula in the relevant
chapter.

CHAPTER

Divisor, character, and the meaning of the
square root
The diagonal Humbert divisor is
𝐻1 = {𝑍∈ℍ2 : 𝑧= 0}/Sp4(ℤ).
Theorem 138.1 (Diagonal divisor). The divisor of 𝐷5 is the Sp4(ℤ)-orbit of 𝐻1, with multiplicity one. The
form transforms by the Maass character 𝜈5 of order two, and 𝜒10 = 𝐷52 is scalar-valued.
Proof. This is the defining diagonal-divisor property of the Igusa square root and the first row of the Cléry–
Gritsenko classification [16,44,45].
Real roots of the denominator algebra define Weyl reflections. Imaginary roots contribute root
spaces and product exponents. This division of labor is intrinsic to a Borcherds algebra: the Weyl group
is generated by real simple roots, while the imaginary simple roots enter the correction term of the
denominator identity.
138.1
Automorphic section, orientation line, and Pfaffian operator
An automorphic square root is a section of a line bundle with character. An orientation is a square root
of a determinant line. A Pfaffian operator is a skew family whose regularized Pfaffian is a section of that
orientation line. These form a sequence of successive refinements:
automorphic section −→determinant-line identification
−→oriented square-root line −→Fredholm family and Pfaffian section.
Proposition 138.2 (Logical separation of the four levels). The product theorem constructs the ﬁrst object
in the sequence. A determinant-line comparison constructs the second. A coherent square root of that line
constructs the third. A ﬁrst-order operator requires a symbol, domain, ellipticity or Fredholm condition,
and regularization, and thereby produces the fourth reﬁnement.
Proof. Each stage adds a specified geometric structure. A determinant realization adds an isomorphism from a
geometric determinant line to the automorphic line. An oriented realization adds a coherent square-root line and
its parity refinement. An operator realization adds a principal symbol, a Fredholm domain, a spectrum, and a
regularization whose Pfaffian is the resulting section.
The title names the monic automorphic section 𝐷5, characterized by the exact identity 𝐷52 = 𝜒10.
Part IV formulates an analytic Pfaffian realization as a further geometric refinement.

The Gritsenko–Nikulin Lie superalgebra
The Borcherds product determines a generalized Kac–Moody Lie superalgebra 𝔤Δ5 with real simple
roots 𝛿1, 𝛿2, 𝛿3 and an imaginary correction system determined by 𝜙0,1.
Theorem 140.1 (Root supermultiplicities and denominator). On the active positive support,
smult𝔤Δ5 𝛼(𝑛,ℓ,𝑚) = 𝑓(𝑛𝑚, ℓ).
In the Lorentzian coordinate convention of Gritsenko–Nikulin,
den(𝔤Δ5) = 𝐷5(2𝑍) = 2−6Δ𝜃
5 (2𝑍).
Proof. TherealrootsandWeylchamberarethetype-IIcaseoftheGritsenko–Nikulinconstruction. Theimaginary
simple-root correction is chosen so that its Weyl–Kac–Borcherds product has exponent 𝑓(𝑛𝑚, ℓ). The resulting
product is the doubled-variable monic Igusa form [38–40].
The theorem gives a Lie superalgebra with a bracket, Cartan, Weyl group, root parity, and denomina-
tor. The scalar product alone records the signed dimensions of the root spaces; the Gritsenko–Nikulin
construction supplies the algebraic operations.

Theorem 142.2 (Finite-height BF realization). The action 𝑆𝐻satisﬁes the classical master equation and

makes every even adjoint endomorphism strictly height-increasing and every odd adjoint endomorphism
odd; hence the modular supercharacter vanishes and the reduced theory satisﬁes the quantum master equa-
tion.

Point-local and wrapped sectors
Let 𝑝𝐸: 𝑆× 𝐸→𝐸be projection.
Lemma 145.1 (Positive elliptic degree is wrapped). Let𝐶⊂𝑆× 𝐸be a properone-dimensional subscheme
whose pushforward ( 𝑝𝐸)∗[𝐶] has positive degree. Then 𝑝𝐸(𝐶) = 𝐸.
Proof. Properness makes 𝑝𝐸(𝐶) closed. Positive pushforward degree implies that at least one irreducible compo-
nent of 𝐶dominates a one-dimensional closed subset of 𝐸. Every one-dimensional closed subset of the irreducible
curve 𝐸is 𝐸itself, so the image of that component, and hence the image of 𝐶, is all of 𝐸.
Projection-finite objects define point-supported sectors on Ran(𝐸). Positive elliptic degree defines
wrapped sectors supported over the entire elliptic curve.
Definition 145.2 (The local–wrapped factorization operad). Let Ch𝐸denote the chosen chi-
ral/factorization operad on 𝐸and let Ass denote the ordered associative operad. Define the colored
operad Hyb𝐸with colors
l,
w𝑚
(𝑚≥1)
by the following operation objects.
(i) Operations with local output are
Hyb𝐸(l⊗𝑘; l) = Ch𝐸(𝑘).
(ii) For 𝑟≥1 and 𝑚= 𝑚1 + · · · + 𝑚𝑟, operations with wrapped output are
Hyb𝐸(l⊗𝑘, w𝑚1, . . . , w𝑚𝑟; w𝑚) = Ch𝐸(𝑘) ⊗Ass(𝑟).
All other operation objects are initial.
Operadic composition uses chiral insertion and disjoint union in the first factor and ordered substitution
in the second. The equality
(𝛾1 ◦𝛾2) ⊗(𝑎1 ◦𝑎2) = (𝛾1 ⊗𝑎1) ◦(𝛾2 ⊗𝑎2)
is the mixed interchange law. The arities (𝑘, 𝑟) = (𝑘, 0), (0, 2), (𝑘, 1) recover, respectively, local factor-
ization, wrapped multiplication, and the action of local insertions on a wrapped sector.

Definition 147.1 (Height-admissible Lie superbialgebra). A root-graded Lie superbialgebra is height-
admissible when every root space is finite-dimensional, every bounded-height region contains finitely
many roots, and the bracket, cobracket, invariant tensor, and all iterated operations are coefficientwise
finite in the height completion.
Proposition 147.2 (Height admissibility). The paired Gritsenko–Nikulin Borcherds Lie superbialgebra
𝔤Δ5
pair is height-admissible.
Proof. The Borcherds root spaces are finite-dimensional and the chosen height is proper. The bracket and co-
bracket preserve total root degree. In a bounded total height, finitely many decompositions of a root occur. The
canonical tensor is therefore a coefficientwise finite sum in every height quotient.

Theorem 148.1 (Classical local and exact zero-mode realization). For every 𝐻:
(i) 𝑆𝐻satisﬁes the classical master equation and deﬁnes a classical holomorphic–topological factoriza-
tion algebra;
(ii) harmonic reduction along a compact interval gives the ﬁnite-dimensional cotangent BV theory of
𝔫Δ5,+(𝐻), whose positive-root grading makes its modular supercharacter zero and hence gives the
quantum master equation;
(iii) after choosing the complementary Lagrangian boundary polarizations, the boundary algebra is
𝑈(𝔫Δ5,+(𝐻)), the interval amplitude is 1 ⊗𝕃
𝑈(𝔫Δ5,+ (𝐻) ) 1 ≃𝐶∗(𝔫Δ5,+(𝐻)), and the holomorphic

height.
The theorem gives the precise field-theoretic realization used in this volume: a classical local theory

𝚥∗(𝑒−𝜌Ber(D∞)) = 𝐷5(𝑍),
𝐷5(𝑍)2 = 𝜒10(𝑍).
Proof. The Berezinian of a diagonal endomorphism is the product of its eigenvalues with exponent equal to the
superdimension of the eigenspace. Proper height makes every coefficient stationary. The denominator theorem
supplies the Weyl line and the root-to-Siegel coordinate map.

CHAPTER
150
What the automorphic character
determines
Theorem 150.1 (Denominator data and reconstruction data). The Borcherds product determines:
(i) the signed root multiplicity sdim𝔤Δ5 𝛼in every active root degree;
(ii) the Weyl vector and denominator character;
(iii) the divisor and automorphic multiplier of the section.
A Lie bracket is supplied by the Borcherds–Kac–Moody presentation, parity dimensions by a parity-reﬁned
root datum, Hall multiplication by extension correspondences, and an oriented square root by a square-root
line with monodromy. These reconstruction data reﬁne the scalar product independently.
Proof. The firstthree items are readfrom the exponent,prefactor,andautomorphylaw. Independence is witnessed
by explicit pairs: two brackets on the same graded vector space have the same PBW character; an even–odd pair has
signed dimension zero; distinct Hall correspondences may have the same Euler product; and twisting a square-root
line by a sign local system preserves its square. Hence each refined object is reconstructed from the additional
datum named in the statement.
Recognition Theorem 150.2 (Complete primitive recognition). Let 𝔭be a positive graded Lie superal-
gebra and let Ψ : 𝔫Δ5,+ ↠𝔭be a graded Lie map. The map is an isomorphism if either:
(i) the parity-reﬁned Hilbert series of 𝑈(𝔫Δ5,+) and 𝑈(𝔭) agree; or
(ii) each primitive degree has prescribed pure parity and the signed PBW characters agree.
The induced height completions, Chevalley complexes, and current envelopes are then isomorphic.
Proof. Choose the first height containing a homogeneous kernel vector. All decomposable PBW terms in that
height are determined by lower heights and agree. The primitive parity dimension therefore drops, contradicting
the first hypothesis. In the second case the logarithm of the PBW product recovers signed primitive dimensions
inductively, and prescribed parity recovers ordinary dimensions. Naturality of completion, Chevalley chains, and
current envelopes gives the final assertion.

(i) ΓΔ,𝐻is a complete three-Calabi–Yau dg algebra;
(ii) the degree-zero inclusion 𝑘𝑄Δ,𝐻→ΓΔ,𝐻is functorial in full-subquiver inclusions;

Coordinate-framed orientation on finite
critical charts
Fix 𝐻and a dimension vectord. The representation space of the graded doubled quiverunderlying ΓΔ,𝐻
is affine. Orderits vertex,arrow,reverse-arrow,and degree-2 loop coordinates. The corresponding volume
line is trivial. The derived moment-map equations form a symmetric three-Calabi–Yau deformation
complex whose determinant is the square of this ordered coordinate line.
Theorem 156.1 (Multiplicative coordinate-framed orientation). A choice of order for the vertex, arrow,
reverse-arrow, and loop coordinates trivializes the ambient canonical line and supplies an orientation of
every ﬁnite-dimensional critical representation chart of ΓΔ,𝐻. Under direct sum and the Hall extension cor-
respondence, the orientation line is the tensor product of the two diagonal orientation lines. The construction
is associative for three summands and compatible with full-subquiver inclusions.
Proof. On the extension chart, every arrow matrix is block upper triangular. The tangent complex splits into two
diagonal deformation complexes and two off-diagonal complexes exchanged by three-Calabi–Yau duality. The
paired off-diagonal part carries the standard determinant square root induced by the three-Calabi–Yau duality
pairing. The ordered diagonal coordinate lines therefore multiply. A three-block decomposition gives the same
tensor order in both parenthesizations. Full-subquiver inclusion appends an ordered set of coordinates.
Corollary 156.2 (Oriented critical chart tower). The ﬁnite critical charts carry compatible coordinate-
framed vanishing-cycle complexes. Their Grothendieck groups reﬁne the quiver Hall tower by the three-
Calabi–Yau critical coeﬃcients.
Proof. The chosen coordinate framings are compatible with direct sum, extension correspondences, and full-
subquiver inclusions in the fixed root order. Vanishing cycles are functorial for these oriented critical charts, so
their convolution complexes and Grothendieck groups form the stated tower.

Universal property
A presentation-preserving protected realization consists of an oriented root-graded theory 𝔗, a map
from each Igusa simple-root space to its primitive states, and chosen comparison maps from the Hall,
Ginzburg, current, quantum-double, and determinant functors applied to that simple-root map.
Theorem 163.1 (Universal presentation map). Every presentation-preserving protected realization carries
a unique Lie-superalgebra morphism
𝔫Δ5,+ −→Prim(𝔗)
extending the simple-root map. Functoriality produces compatible morphisms from the Hall algebra, the
Ginzburg completion, the local–wrapped current theory, the completed quantum group, and the virtual
root determinant of 𝔎Δ to their selected targets in 𝔗. These morphisms are equivalences when the primitive
map and the selected functorial comparison maps are equivalences.
Proof. The Borcherds generators and relations give the unique primitive Lie morphism. Hall convolution, Cal-
abi–Yau completion, chiral Chevalley chains, quantized doubling, and the diagonal Fredholm construction are
functorial in the root presentation. PBW promotes a primitive equivalence to the completed enveloping and Fock
objects. The remaining equivalences are exactly the selected comparison maps in the definition of a presentation-
preserving realization.

Concordance with the earlier Igusa draft
The reconstitution retains the half-index determinant, theta normalization, Borcherds denominator,
reduced scalar theorem, charge Gram map, and geometric Hall comparison datum. It reorganizes their
dependencies as follows:
(i) the automorphic square root is completed entirely within the Borcherds product theorem;
(ii) the BKM bracket comes from the Gritsenko–Nikulin algebra;
(iii) the bar–Chevalley comparison gives the denominator a homological source;
(iv) the current and BF constructions give an exact first-order algebraic model;
(v) orientation data refine determinant lines, while an operator Pfaffian requires an actual Fredholm
family;
(vi) Weyl reflections are attached to real roots, and imaginary roots enter through multiplicities;
(vii) the compact Hall comparison uses additive Mukai charge, the polarized central extension, and
hybrid local–wrapped sectors.

Perturbative factorization realization
Choose elliptic gauge-fixing data for 𝔩full

The Koszul–Morita square
Let 𝑃be a dualizable boundary state for 𝐴⊥. Its boundary dual is
𝐴!
𝑃= RHom𝐴⊥(𝑃, 𝑃).
The two-sided bar sends the diagonal bimodule to a diagonal bicomodule.
Theorem 176.1 (Mixed Koszul–Morita transport). On the positive complete locus, the universal twisting
cochains identify complete mixed modules with compatible comodules over the four bar objects. Derived
tensor products of interfaces become derived cotensor products. The mixed centre and mixed Hochschild
chains are preserved.
Proof. Apply one-colour bar–cobar duality to each associative colour. The interface bar is a bimodule bar and
its filtration is finite in each total weight. The aligned distributive law identifies the two orders of transformation.
Eilenberg–Moore comparison sends tensor to cotensor. Endomorphisms of the diagonal and its trace are invariant
under the induced Morita equivalence.

Planar exchange and Tannaka

A second topological direction gives paths exchanging parallel defects. Their homotopy classes form

continuous dual acts on every line object. The meromorphic braiding gives the universal 𝑅-matrix on
completed matrix coeﬃcients. A spectral rational braiding yields an RTT presentation.
Proof. Tensor product in L gives the coproduct on matrix coefficients. Evaluation gives the counit. Rigidity gives
the antipode on the Hopf envelope. The braiding defines the coquasitriangular pairing, and the hexagon identities
give the Yang–Baxter equation. Writing this naturality on a generating fibre produces the RTT relation.

CHAPTER
181
Four equivalent linear complexes
Fix a constant-curvature solution (𝑒, 𝜔). There are four deformation complexes.
(i) The flat adjoint complex Ω•(𝑀, 𝔤Λ) with differential 𝑑A.
(ii) The Einstein–Cartan complex on (𝛿𝑒, 𝛿𝜔) with linearized torsion and curvature.
(iii) The metric Einstein complex on ℎ𝜇𝜈with linearized Einstein operator.
(iv) The Calabi complex resolving local Killing fields by the Killing, linearized curvature, and Bianchi
operators.
Theorem 181.1 (Chain equivalence of gravitational presentations). An invertible torsion-free dreibein
gives chain maps among the four complexes. These maps are quasi-isomorphisms on every geodesically
convex open set. Their cohomology describes inﬁnitesimal isometries, physical linearized ﬁelds, and Bianchi
identities in the four languages.
Proof. The dreibein identifies internal translations with tangent vectors and internal Lorentz rotations with
skew endomorphisms. Splitting 𝑑A in this basis gives the linearized torsion and curvature operators. Eliminating
𝛿𝜔by the linearized torsion equation gives the metric Einstein operator. The symbol sequence is the Calabi
symbol sequence and is exact on constant-curvature space. The Poincaré lemma for the flat adjoint connection
and exactness of the symbol homotopies give local quasi-isomorphisms.

EinBV3(𝐴𝐿, 𝐴𝑅) = 𝑇∗[−1] MC 𝔤grav ⋉Ham 𝔪𝐴𝐿,𝐴𝑅

Theorem 183.2 (Einstein completion theorem). The completion has a canonical BV action. Its pure

gravitational summand gives the Cartan action. The mixed term is the Hamiltonian pairing of the gauge field with
the matter moment map.

One-loop gravity and analytic torsion
Expand Chern–Simons theory around an acyclic flat connection A. Gauge fixing gives the Laplacians
of the flat adjoint de Rham complex.
Theorem 188.1 (One-loop determinant). The absolute value of the one-loop partition function is the
square root of the Ray–Singer torsion of the ﬂat adjoint bundle:
|𝑍1-loop(A)| = 𝜏RS(𝑀, ad A)1/2.
The phase is the eta-invariant contribution ﬁxed by the framing convention.
Proof. Bosonic and ghost Gaussian integrals give alternating determinants of the Laplacians in the flat de Rham
complex. Their product is the analytic torsion. The odd signature operator supplies the determinant phase. This
is the standard stationary-phase calculation for Chern–Simons theory [66,71].
The same determinant complex is obtained from the Einstein–Cartan, metric, and Calabi pre-
sentations through their chain equivalences. One-loop equality is therefore a consequence of quasi-
isomorphism together with compatible determinant-line orientations.

Four native anomaly classes
The construction carries four curvature classes.
(i) A local BV anomaly lies in the degree-one local deformation complex.
(ii) A projective anomaly is the curvature of the determinant line over parameter space.
(iii) A framing anomaly is the transgression of a characteristic class to a three-manifold.
(iv) A curved Koszul term is the arity-zero component of a curved coalgebra.
Theorem 191.1 (Curvature-killing extension). Let 𝜅be a closed central degree-two class in the deformation
complex of a theory. Adjoining a degree-one generator 𝑢with 𝑑𝑢= 𝜅produces a central extension in which
the liftedcurvature vanishes. Tensoring withthe inverse invertible ﬁeldtheory gives the geometric realization
of the same cancellation.
Proof. The extended differential squares to zero because 𝑑𝜅= 0 and 𝜅is central. The new Maurer–Cartan
equation contains the compensating field 𝑢. An invertible theory with curvature −𝜅adds the opposite class under
tensor product.

Thus every subtheory, every intersection of subtheories, and the complete theory are values of one descent
object.

Proof. An inclusion 𝑆⊆𝑇induces the full inclusion I𝑆⊆I𝑇and therefore the restriction map on homotopy
limits. Composition of inclusions gives functoriality. A relation-complete cover presents I𝑈as the displayed
homotopy colimit of its intersection diagram. Mapping that colimit into the diagram of structure spaces converts
it into the stated homotopy limit. The resulting equivalence is natural under refinement of the carrier cover.

CHAPTER
195
Sixteen structural laws
The theory is governed by the following laws.
(i) A geometric carrier determines the type of its operation.
(ii) The diagonal stores the complete principal-part filtration.
(iii) The interval stores ordered fusion.
(iv) The plane stores exchange.
(v) The circle stores trace.
(vi) The stable curve stores modular sewing.
(vii) The transverse, associative-chiral, and chiral-Lie bars are distinct.
(viii) Chiral PBW compares the last two on enveloping objects.
(ix) The interface bar records the mixed distributive law.
(x) The derived centre is the universal interior action.
(xi) The deformation algebra is the tangent algebra of the centre-extended datum.
(xii) Maurer–Cartan curvature is the field equation.
(xiii) The 𝐿∞identity is the Bianchi identity.
(xiv) Shifted cotangent is the universal classical BV envelope.
(xv) Hodge-completed derived de Rham theory is the intrinsic algebraic quantum complex of the
oriented formal moduli problem.
(xvi) Einstein completion is projective matching followed by gravitational cotangent transgression.

The construction proceeds by a single chain of functors:

Hall and Calabi–Yau sources enter on the left. Quantum groups enter through planar exchange and the
line category. Automorphic products enter as characters of root-graded protected objects. Each arrow
preserves the structure carried by its source and adds the structure specified by its geometric target.

Low-arity mixed signs
Let 𝑥|𝑘|𝑦be a suspended interface word. The two length-lowering contractions are
𝑏𝐿[𝑥|𝑘|𝑦] = (−1)|𝑥|(𝑥𝑘)|𝑦,
𝑏𝑅[𝑥|𝑘|𝑦] = (−1)|𝑥|+|𝑘|𝑥|(𝑘𝑦).
Applying the remaining contraction gives the two composites (𝑥𝑘)𝑦and 𝑥(𝑘𝑦) with opposite
determinant-line signs. Their sum vanishes by the interface associativity relation. A transverse and a
chiral contraction acquire the product orientation
det(𝐸ch ⊕𝐸⊥) = det(𝐸ch) ⊗det(𝐸⊥),
so reversing their order contributes the Koszul sign and yields anticommutation.

The theorem of the whole construction
Theorem 200.1 (Platonic Calabi–Yau quantum-group theorem). For every integral Calabi–Yau
quantum-group datum 𝔇, the following diagram is deﬁned and commutes up to its canonical coherent
homotopies:
𝐶𝐶∗(C)

A character functor sends the lower row to the corresponding Hall, PBW, Fock, determinant, or automor-
phic series. An open–closed complete boundary identiﬁes the middle vertical map with an equivalence. A
presentation-complete Hall comparison identiﬁes the lower right quantum group with the quantum group
of the geometric BPS sector.
Proof. The upper row is the formal-to-geometric realization and boundary restriction. The left vertical arrow is
shifted-symplectic transgression followed by the oriented extension correspondence. The middle vertical arrow is
the open–closed action. The lower row is Hall convolution followed by the paired Drinfeld double. Compatibility
is the base-change and naturality content of the assembly theorem. The character statement follows from PBW and
trace functoriality. The two equivalence clauses are the defining recognition criteria for open–closed completeness
and presentation completeness.

CHAPTER
201
The universal K3 quantum algebra
Let 𝑆be a K3 surface. The Nakajima Heisenberg correspondences, the Looijenga–Lunts–Verbitsky
action, and the local ADE Yangian correspondences act on distinct geometric towers. Their common
action algebra is defined as the complete algebra generated by their kernels inside the endomorphisms of
the direct sum of these towers.
Theorem 201.1 (K3 action-envelope theorem). The completed algebra generated by the Heisenberg, LLV,
and local ADE kernels exists as a quotient of their completed amalgamated free product over the common
Cartanandgradingoperators. Itisinitialamongcompletealgebrasreceivingthethreegeometricactionswith
their actual mixed commutators. Its Tannaka coend reconstructs the corresponding coordinate bialgebra
whenever the generated module category is rigid and carries a faithful continuous ﬁbre functor.
Proof. Complete filtered algebras admit degreewise amalgamated free products. Quotient by the kernel of the
combinedactionmaptotheendomorphismalgebraofthegeometrictower. Thisquotienthastheasserteduniversal
property. Under rigidity and a faithful fibre functor, the coend construction gives the coordinate bialgebra and
recovers the action category.
This object gives the phrase ‘‘K3 quantum algebra’’ a precise universal meaning. It preserves the three
established geometric sources and stores their mixed commutators instead of identifying them by rank.
519




CHAPTER
202
The central compact example
For 𝑋= 𝐾3 × 𝐸, the universal Igusa object supplies the algebraic Hall–Borcherds target, its quan-
tum group, its local–wrapped current object, and its automorphic line. A reduced oriented compact
Hall source, when supplied, is compared with this target by the geometric recognition theorem. Thus
the formal, algebraic, analytic, and automorphic layers are closed; the compact geometric content is
concentrated in a single explicit realization datum.
Theorem 202.1 (Universal–geometricequivalencecriterion). LetGeo𝐾3×𝐸beareducedoriented 𝐾3 × 𝐸
Hall datum with polarized Gram grading. A comparison
ΦBPS : Prim(Geo𝐾3×𝐸) −→𝔤Δ5+
extends to an equivalence of completed doubles, current factorization objects, centres, and determinant lines
exactly when:
(i) it maps the geometric simple states to the real and imaginary simple generators;
(ii) it is surjective on primitives;
(iii) its parity-reﬁned PBW character equals the Igusa root character;
(iv) it intertwines the coproduct, Hopf pairing, local–wrapped actions, and orientation line.
Proof. The first condition gives a presentation morphism. The second and third give a primitive isomorphism by
minimal-height PBW recognition. The fourth transports the additional structures. Completion is exact in every
proper-height degree, hence the equivalence passes to the inverse limit.
521




CHAPTER
203
Coda: geometry becomes algebra and
preserves its origin
A Calabi–Yau category supplies duality. A BV theory supplies locality. A boundary supplies order. A
secondtransverse direction suppliesexchange. Amoduli stacksuppliesstates. Extension correspondences
supply multiplication. A pairing supplies the double. A trace supplies a character. A Borcherds lift
supplies an automorphic denominator. The integrated datum preserves the source of every operation.
This preservation is the simplicity of the subject: each geometric motion has one algebraic image, and
the whole theory is their functorial composition.
